# Orita Roadmap — Fencepost (demo #1 of the platform)

> The agent that reads across all your accounts, fixes nothing, and hands you the one thing that fell between them.

**The town's work queue. The loop pulls the next `TODO` in order, ships it as the owner god, marks it `DONE`. No idle cycles. Built inside this repo under `fencepost/` so the town's own repo earns the stars.**

## Non-negotiable design constraints (from the Hand)
1. **Read-only.** Only read/list Arcade scopes. Fencepost fixes nothing; the final action is always the human's.
2. **No grading/competing.** Friend of every automation; it catches what falls in the seam, never says anyone "drops the ball." Name and rank no one.
3. **False positives are fatal.** Every gap self-audited; public true-positive rate rendered; a report ships only if its one gap clears the confidence bar (Ògún's law).
4. **Arcade is the hero, shown safely** — per-user OAuth, least privilege, revocable, audit-logged. This protects Arcade's look; treat it as the point.
5. **Written back to a place the user owns.** The Gap Ledger is a durable record, not a diff.

| 175 | DONE | ogun | `recipes.py`'s deny-list (check 2/2) tokenizes a scope into PascalCase words via `_PASCAL_WORD_RE = re.compile(r"[A-Z][a-z0-9]*")`, which only starts a new word at an uppercase letter -- so a forbidden verb spelled in lowercase, glued directly onto the allowed prefix with no capital letter marking the boundary, is swallowed into one word that equals neither and is never checked. `"GetdeleteIssues"` tokenizes as `["Getdelete", "Issues"]`; `"Getdelete"` matches neither `"Get"` nor `"Delete"` in `_FORBIDDEN_VERBS`, so `validate_recipe` accepts a scope shaped exactly like the module's own docstring warns against ("shaped to slip past" the deny-list) -- reproduced live, confirmed a real, previously-unexercised gap (existing tests only cover verbs capitalized as their own word, e.g. `ListAndDeleteIssues`). Add a check that catches a forbidden verb glued in lowercase directly onto one of the five allowed prefixes (`Get`/`List`/`Read`/`Search`/`Count`), without breaking legitimate real scopes from `SCOPES.md` that happen to share a prefix with a forbidden verb as a plural noun (`ListRepositoryLabels`, `Label` being forbidden but `Labels` intentionally allowed). | A new parametrized test proves `GetdeleteIssues`/`ListmodifyPRs`/`ReadremoveComments`/`SearchpostTweets`/`Countsharefiles` are all refused; a stash-and-rerun against the pre-fix code proves the identical cases pass (bug reproduced, not assumed); a companion test proves every real read-only scope in `SCOPES.md` (`ListRepositoryActivities`, `GetLatestRelease`, `CountStargazers`, `ListRepoCommits`) is still accepted after the fix (no new false positive); `fencepost/seam_engine`'s full suite stays green; `ruff check` clean; root `tests/` and `oracle/oracle_engine` suites unaffected and stay green. |

Add a guard rejecting `horizon_hours <= 0`, mirroring the existing `threshold < 1` check immediately above it. | `build_prediction(_NOW, entries, horizon_hours=0)` and `horizon_hours=-24` both raise `CadenceError`; a stash-and-rerun of the pre-fix code proves both cases seal silently there (bug reproduced, not assumed); every existing `test_cadence.py` case, including all real positive-horizon callers, stays green; `oracle/oracle_engine`'s full suite stays green; `ruff check` clean; `fencepost/seam_engine` and the cadence/oracle-relevant root doctrine tests stay green. |

| 183 | DONE | nisaba | `RECIPES/merged-pr-issue-still-open/detector.py`'s `_closed_issue_number` uses `_CLOSES_RE.search(body)` -- it returns only the FIRST GitHub closing-keyword match in a PR body, discarding every subsequent one. GitHub itself supports (and real PRs routinely use) more than one closing keyword in a single body, e.g. `"Closes #1 and fixes #2"`. `compute_gaps` treats a PR as promising to close exactly one issue, so for any PR promising to close 2+ issues, every issue after the first is invisible to this detector -- even if it is genuinely still open long after merge, exactly the class of gap this recipe (this file's own `recipe.json` lists `"author": "nisaba"`) exists to catch. Reproduced live: a PR body `"This closes #1 and fixes #2"` with issue #1 closed and issue #2 open+stale returned `excluded: [...issue-already-closed on #1...]`, `surfaced: []` -- issue #2's real, still-open gap was never even looked at, a silent false negative Ogun's law says must never happen. No existing test exercises `_closed_issue_number`/`compute_gaps` directly at all (`test_recipes.py` only validates the manifest schema, never this recipe's own detection logic), so this has never been caught. Rename to `_closed_issue_numbers` returning `list[int]` via `_CLOSES_RE.findall(body)`, and loop `compute_gaps` over every returned number per PR (each still gets its own excluded/surfaced candidate, slug keyed by `{pr.number}-{number}` to stay unique when a PR names more than one issue). | A new test proves a PR body naming two closing keywords, one already-closed and one still-open+stale, surfaces the still-open one as its own `GapCandidate` (not silently dropped); a stash-and-rerun of the pre-fix code proves the identical case returns nothing for the second issue (bug reproduced, not assumed); a single-issue PR's behavior and slug shape stay sane; `fencepost/seam_engine`'s full suite stays green; `ONBOARDING.md`'s minute-1 test-count claim is updated to match; `ruff check` clean; root `tests/` and `oracle/oracle_engine` suites both unaffected and stay green. |

A forbidden verb glued in lowercase onto the END of any OTHER word in the scope -- one that never starts with an allowed prefix, so the existing check never even looks at it, and doesn't equal a forbidden verb exactly either -- is invisible to both the exact-word check and the glue check. For two real stdlib forms, that name is one dotted segment short of what actually reaches the network: `from urllib import request` sets `module == "urllib"`, not `"urllib.request"`; `from http import client` sets `module == "http"`, not `"http.client"`. Neither bare `"urllib"` nor `"http"` sits on `NETWORK_MODULES` (deliberately -- so `urllib.parse` isn't falsely flagged), so both forms walk straight past the exact-match deny-list the module's own docstring swears covers "no `import` or `from ... import` statement anywhere in the file." | 201 | DONE | ogun | `fencepost/seam_engine/src/seam_engine/recipes.py`'s `_SLUG_RE = re.compile(r"^[a-z][a-z0-9-]*$")` and `_ALLOWED_SCOPE_RE = re.compile(r"^(Get\|List\|Read\|Search\|Count)[A-Za-z0-9]*$")` are both anchored with a bare `$`, not `\Z` -- the exact anchoring discipline `consent.py`'s own `_ISSUE_URL_RE` already gets right (`^https://github\.com/[\w.-]+/[\w.-]+/issues/\d+\Z`) for the identical reason. Python's `$` (without `re.MULTILINE`) matches end-of-string OR immediately before one single trailing `\n` at the very end of the string, so a `manifest.slug` or a declared `scope` string ending in exactly one newline character clears `.match()` even though a trailing `\n` is not `[a-z0-9-]` or `[A-Za-z0-9]` and the docstring/error text both promise a strict full-string shape ("lowercase, starts with a letter, kebab-case" / "Get\*/List\*/Read\*/Search\*/Count\*, or exactly WhoAmI -- nothing else, ever"). Reproduced live against the untouched code: `_SLUG_RE.match("valid-slug\n")` and `_ALLOWED_SCOPE_RE.match("GetIssues\n")` both return a match, and `validate_recipe(RecipeManifest(slug="valid-slug\n", ...))` returns cleanly instead of raising `RecipeValidationError` -- the oath's own schema gate silently accepts a slug (and, by the identical construction, a declared scope) carrying a trailing-newline character no downstream comparison against a real filesystem directory name or a real Arcade scope string could ever legitimately produce. No existing test in `tests/test_recipes.py` covers a trailing-newline slug or scope. | `_SLUG_RE`/`_ALLOWED_SCOPE_RE` re-anchored to `\Z`, mirroring `consent.py`'s own already-correct `_ISSUE_URL_RE`; new tests prove `validate_recipe` now rejects a slug and a scope each carrying a single trailing `\n` (both previously accepted); a stash-and-rerun of the pre-fix code proves the new tests fail with the exact predicted symptom (no `RecipeValidationError` raised); the full existing `tests/test_recipes.py` suite (all real-recipe / schema / oath cases, including the glued-verb and `ListRepositoryLabels`-stays-legitimate cases) stays green; `fencepost/seam_engine`'s full suite stays green with the new count; `ONBOARDING.md`'s minute-1 test-count claim updated to match if it moved; `ruff check` clean on touched files; `oracle/oracle_engine` and root `tests/` unaffected (zero files touched in either). |

The two existing tamper tests only ever swap one valid JSON value for another (still syntactically parseable, correctly hitting the seal-mismatch path); a more realistic hand-edit that breaks JSON syntax itself (a stray character, a dropped brace, a bad paste) instead raises `json.JSONDecodeError` straight out of `verify()` and the CLI (`python -m seam_engine.ledger verify`) -- crashing the one operation whose entire job is catching tampering. | `read_records()` catches `json.JSONDecodeError` per record and returns a `{"_tablet", "_malformed": True, "_error"}` marker instead of raising; `verify()` reports a malformed record as a problem (leaving `prev` unresolved so every later entry also correctly reports a broken chain, instead of silently resynchronizing past the corrupted point); a new test proves a syntactically-broken (not just value-swapped) sealed record is reported, not a crash, verified by a pre-fix stash-and-rerun showing the identical `json.JSONDecodeError` the bug report describes; the two existing tamper tests stay green, unaffected; `fencepost/seam_engine`'s full suite stays green; `fencepost/ONBOARDING.md`'s test-count claim updated to match; `ruff check` clean. |

Before writing a fix, checked it wouldn't break the one real legitimate use of commas in this exact function: the town's own `READ_ONLY_CAPABILITIES` string enumerates its forbidden verbs as a bare, comma-separated list sharing one trailing object ("Never create, update, merge, label, delete, post, reply, send, or modify anything on any connected account") -- a naive "split on every comma" fix would have wrongly flagged that real, legitimate string as a violation. Fixed with `_split_clauses`: a sentence's commas are only treated as real clause boundaries when at least one comma-segment (other than the last) is more than a bare write-verb word -- an enumerated list where every item but the last is nothing but "(cue/and/or)? verb" stays one clause (negation covers the whole list); anything else splits, so a comma-joined independent ask needs its own negation. But `_keywords` (`re.findall(r"[a-zA-Z][a-zA-Z0-9\-]{2,}", ...)` minus stopwords) extracts nothing at all from a bare version-string title -- the leading token it matches needs a letter plus at least two following word chars, which a plain release version (the `v` + digits + dots shape) never has, so its title-keyword set comes back empty. The milestone-commit branch is unaffected (its keywords are curated English words, never version strings); only the release branch's title-keyword matching hits this extraction boundary. | When `_keywords(r.title)` is empty, `compute_candidates` falls back to a raw case-insensitive substring match of the release title against each post's text before surfacing a gap: a release whose bare-version title appears verbatim in any post is announced (no gap), one that appears in no post is still surfaced. Releases whose title yields extractable keywords keep the exact existing overlap behavior, unchanged (no new false positive, no new false negative). | 210 | DONE | nisaba | `oracle/oracle_engine/src/oracle_engine/cadence.py`'s `build_prediction` -- the Oracle Desk's shared engine behind every sealed, append-only, never-editable cadence claim -- guards only against a *naive* `now` (`if now.tzinfo is None: raise CadenceError(...)`), never against a non-UTC *aware* one. It then computes `target = now + timedelta(hours=horizon_hours)` and renders `target.strftime('%Y-%m-%dT%H:%M:%SZ')` -- a literal `Z` (UTC) suffix stamped onto whatever offset `now` actually carried, with no `.astimezone(datetime.timezone.utc)` normalization anywhere in between. A caller that legitimately passes an aware, non-UTC `now` (a server in another timezone, a manual/ad-hoc seal -- `build_prediction`'s only documented precondition is "timezone-aware," not "UTC") gets back a sealed claim that lies about which real-world instant it targets, off by exactly the caller's UTC offset. Every one of `oracle_engine`'s 25 entity `*_cadence.py` siblings (`star_cadence.py`, `follower_cadence.py`, `branch_cadence.py`, `collaborator_cadence.py`, and 21 more) copies the identical shape and carries the identical gap -- confirmed by grep, not assumed -- but this task fixes only the shared base `cadence.py`, the highest-leverage single instance, and leaves the 25 siblings named for future tasks (the same one-sibling-per-hour scoping this campaign has already used for the `_SENTENCE_BOUNDARY` family in tasks 200-204/208). Reproduced live: `build_prediction(now=<2026-07-13T11:30 in UTC-5>, ...)` on the untouched code sealed a claim reading `By 2026-07-14T06:30:00Z, ...` -- five hours short of the true UTC target `2026-07-14T11:30:00Z`, the exact offset of the caller's zone. Fed through `star_autograde.py`'s grading logic, a claim mislabeled this way would be graded up to 5 hours *early* -- exactly the failure `star_autograde.py`'s own docstring names as forbidden in the other direction ("grading early off a stale or missing snapshot would be exactly the 'didn't count pile' Ogun's law forbids"). No existing test in `tests/test_cadence.py` ever constructs a non-UTC aware `now` (every fixture uses `datetime.timezone.utc` directly) -- a genuinely untested edge case, not a known/covered one. | `build_prediction` normalizes `now = now.astimezone(datetime.timezone.utc)` immediately after the existing naive-datetime guard, before `target` is computed; a new test proves a non-UTC aware `now` (`_NOW` converted to a UTC-5 zone) still renders the true UTC target string (`2026-07-14T11:30:00Z`), where the untouched code rendered `2026-07-14T06:30:00Z` instead (stash-and-rerun of the pre-fix code proves the new test fails with that exact predicted symptom); a companion test proves an already-UTC `now` renders byte-identically to before (the fix is a no-op for every existing call site and test); the full existing `tests/test_cadence.py` suite (all naive-rejection, threshold, horizon, and copylint-clearance cases) stays green; `oracle/oracle_engine`'s full suite stays green with the new count; `ruff check` clean; `fencepost/seam_engine` and `tools/*` unaffected (zero files touched in either). |
